## Details

### Coddy — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/coddy_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/coddy_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/coddy)

> Thin wrapper that copies upstream's static Go binary out of its published scratch image onto Alpine (scratch has no shell for an entrypoint). The pin is the image tag = git tag <version>, no v prefix. MIT. See guide §5.27.

### DSH + LongMemory — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/dsh_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/dsh_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-28) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/dsh-longmemory)

> Upstream ships prereleases only (alpha/rc every few days); the pin is an npm version whose git tag is dsh-v<version>. LongMemory is pinned in its own template repo. Sign-in is a generated DSH_GATE_PASSWORD, not the launch token: DSH mints that token with randomBytes() per process and it cannot be preset, so the gateway redeems it behind the edge gate and the deployer only sees a browser password prompt. The gate and auto sign-in are interlocked — clearing the password disables both. The gate and the redirect must live in a Caddy `route`, not a `handle`, or `redir` sorts ahead of `basic_auth` and an anonymous GET / is answered 302 with the live token; test-gateway.sh pins that case, and its stand-in must answer the exchange with the same Location as the pinned DSH (0.1.7 sends ./ where 0.1.5 sent /; the Caddyfile loop guard matches both). See guide §5.22.

  > This release tightens supply-chain verification. Keyless cosign bundles must now match a pinned signer identity, and GitHub attestation workflow checks no longer accept partial matches. It also closes a `.tool-versions` trust gap that could leak `GITHUB_TOKEN`, adds a per-cask `appdir` option for Homebrew casks, and fixes problems with locked SLSA installs, musl hosts and `mise backends switch`.

  > `mise.toml` can now `include` a shared config file from a git repository or OCI registry, and `task_config.includes` accepts OCI artifacts.   > `mise self-update` and the mise.run installer now pick the newest stable release that is at least 24 hours old. Updates also check the release's signed packslip before replacing the binary. This release also adds a machine-local global `miserc`, an opt-in way for command-not-found to install registry tools, and a `postinstall` mode that runs on every install. It fixes several Homebrew formula builds and closes a trust gap in paranoid mode.

  > Aqua tools can now choose glibc or musl builds one tool at a time, and monorepo roots can get short task path aliases. Packslip tools keep installing after their GitHub or GitLab repository is renamed, because mise now pins them by repository ID, recorded in a new lockfile revision 3. SLSA provenance checks now require the expected signer identity. Newer releases (2):
- [v1.4.1](https://github.com/EverMind-AI/EverOS/releases/tag/v1.4.1) 2026-09-24
  > **A fresh install works again.** The `openai` SDK released its 3.x line on
  > 2026-09-24; an unconstrained install picked it up together with an httpx
  > pre-release, and every LLM and embedding call failed before reaching the
  > network. This release pins the SDK to the 2.x line the project is tested
  > against. Nothing else changed since 1.4.0.
  > 
  > ## Fixed
  > 
  > - **`openai` is pinned below 3.** Fresh installs from PyPI resolved
  >   `openai 3.19.2`, whose client raises `AttributeError: module 'httpx' has no
  >   attribute 'Timeout'` on every request, so memorize and hybrid / vector
  >   search returned 500. Existing e …
- [v1.4.0](https://github.com/EverMind-AI/EverOS/releases/tag/v1.4.0) 2026-09-24
  > **EverOS runs natively on Windows, and dense search stops scanning the whole
  > table.** `pip install everos` on a stock Windows 11 machine now works with no
  > manual steps beyond an LLM key; the unit suite runs on Windows in CI. Every
  > vector column gets an IVF_FLAT index once it holds enough rows, built and
  > maintained by the cascade worker, so hybrid search no longer reads the entire
  > column per query. A ten-hour Windows soak drove the rest of this release: a
  > second process can no longer write the index behind a running server, watcher
  > events for one file commit in the order they arrived, and a tra …

- [v0.79.14](https://github.com/inkeep/open-knowledge/releases/tag/v0.79.14) 2026-10-01
  > Honor config lock acquisition deadlines (#5260)
  > 
  > * [wip] claim config lock deadline repair
  > 
  > * Test config lock acquisition deadlines under changing locks
  > 
  > * Honor config lock acquisition deadlines on every retry
  > 
  > * Advance the lock deadline test clock after expiry
  > 
  > The helper froze Date.now one tick past the deadline. A sync lock change
  > that reached the retry busy-wait then never saw time pass, and the test
  > worker exhausted its heap recording spy calls instead of failing an
  > assertion. The clock now stays past the deadline and advances on each read.

### PenguinHarness — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/penguin_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/penguin_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/penguinharness)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image, kept unmodified — its entrypoint already prepares the data root and drops to uid 1000 with exec setpriv. The wrapper adds Railway environment plus a guard that refuses to start without a pinned admin password. The pin is the image tag = git tag with a v prefix. Apache-2.0. See guide §5.28.

- [v2026.10.0](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.10.0) 2026-10-01
  > This release tightens supply-chain verification. Keyless cosign bundles must now match a pinned signer identity, and GitHub attestation workflow checks no longer accept partial matches. It also closes a `.tool-versions` trust gap that could leak `GITHUB_TOKEN`, adds a per-cask `appdir` option for Homebrew casks, and fixes problems with locked SLSA installs, musl hosts and `mise backends switch`.
  > 
  > ## Security
  > 
  > - **Inline tool options in `.tool-versions` now require trust.** This is the `.tool-versions` version of the `mise.toml` fix in 2026.9.18. An untrusted project could ship a `github:` entr …
- [v2026.9.18](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.18) 2026-09-30
  > `mise.toml` can now `include` a shared config file from a git repository or OCI registry, and `task_config.includes` accepts OCI artifacts. The release also closes a trust bypass that could send `GITHUB_TOKEN` to an attacker-controlled host, adds gem registry sources, and fixes several daemon and dotfiles problems.
  > 
  > ## Security
  > 
  > - **Inline tool options now require trust.** Before this change, a `mise.toml` in an untrusted directory could hide options in a tool key, for example a `github:` tool key with `[api_url=...]` pointing at another host. mise loaded the file without trust because the val …
- [v2026.9.17](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.17) 2026-09-29
  > `mise self-update` and the mise.run installer now pick the newest stable release that is at least 24 hours old. Updates also check the release's signed packslip before replacing the binary. This release also adds a machine-local global `miserc`, an opt-in way for command-not-found to install registry tools, and a `postinstall` mode that runs on every install. It fixes several Homebrew formula builds and closes a trust gap in paranoid mode.
  > 
  > ## Changed
  > 
  > - **Self-update and installs wait for a minimum release age.** When no version is pinned, `mise self-update`, automatic updates, update notific …
- [v2026.9.16](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.16) 2026-09-28
  > Aqua tools can now choose glibc or musl builds one tool at a time, and monorepo roots can get short task path aliases. Packslip tools keep installing after their GitHub or GitLab repository is renamed, because mise now pins them by repository ID, recorded in a new lockfile revision 3. SLSA provenance checks now require the expected signer identity. This release also fixes regressions in `mise run --no-timings`, `cargo +nightly` and the `outdated`/`upgrade` version comparison, and speeds up shims and config loading.
  > 
  > ## Added
  > 
  > - **Per-tool `libc` for aqua tools.** On glibc Linux, mise prefers a …
- [v2026.9.15](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.15) 2026-09-27
  > `mise oci build` can now package tools installed by vfox plugins, and vfox plugins can repair an existing install when its tool options change. Shell prompts, `cd`, and settings loading are faster. Dotfiles `include`/`exclude` patterns now follow `.gitignore` rules for `*` and a leading `/`, which fixes a case where rollback could delete a live file.
  > 
  > ## Added

- [v2026.9.14](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.14) 2026-09-25
  > A folder inside any `conf.d` directory now loads as its own config fragment and serves as the config root for the files in it, which gives `[bootstrap].config_roots` users a direct migration path. `[dotfiles]` gains two GNU Stow-style options: relative symlinks and `dot-<name>` sources. Release metadata for any public github.com repo now comes from mise-versions, and the registry can require GitHub attestations for specific tools.

### Scrumboy — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/scrumboy_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/scrumboy_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/scrumboy-1)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image plus curl and tini. The entrypoint claims the Owner account against a loopback-only listener before the public port opens, because Scrumboy gives ownership to the first caller of /api/auth/bootstrap and serves anonymous reads and writes until then. Image tags are semver WITHOUT a v prefix; the git tag has one. AGPL-3.0. See guide \u00a75.29.

- [v2026.10.0](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.10.0) 2026-10-01
  > This release tightens supply-chain verification. Keyless cosign bundles must now match a pinned signer identity, and GitHub attestation workflow checks no longer accept partial matches. It also closes a `.tool-versions` trust gap that could leak `GITHUB_TOKEN`, adds a per-cask `appdir` option for Homebrew casks, and fixes problems with locked SLSA installs, musl hosts and `mise backends switch`.
  > 
  > ## Security
  > 
  > - **Inline tool options in `.tool-versions` now require trust.** This is the `.tool-versions` version of the `mise.toml` fix in 2026.9.18. An untrusted project could ship a `github:` entr …
- [v2026.9.18](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.18) 2026-09-30
  > `mise.toml` can now `include` a shared config file from a git repository or OCI registry, and `task_config.includes` accepts OCI artifacts. The release also closes a trust bypass that could send `GITHUB_TOKEN` to an attacker-controlled host, adds gem registry sources, and fixes several daemon and dotfiles problems.
  > 
  > ## Security
  > 
  > - **Inline tool options now require trust.** Before this change, a `mise.toml` in an untrusted directory could hide options in a tool key, for example a `github:` tool key with `[api_url=...]` pointing at another host. mise loaded the file without trust because the val …
- [v2026.9.17](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.17) 2026-09-29
  > `mise self-update` and the mise.run installer now pick the newest stable release that is at least 24 hours old. Updates also check the release's signed packslip before replacing the binary. This release also adds a machine-local global `miserc`, an opt-in way for command-not-found to install registry tools, and a `postinstall` mode that runs on every install. It fixes several Homebrew formula builds and closes a trust gap in paranoid mode.
  > 
  > ## Changed
  > 
  > - **Self-update and installs wait for a minimum release age.** When no version is pinned, `mise self-update`, automatic updates, update notific …
- [v2026.9.16](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.16) 2026-09-28
  > Aqua tools can now choose glibc or musl builds one tool at a time, and monorepo roots can get short task path aliases. Packslip tools keep installing after their GitHub or GitLab repository is renamed, because mise now pins them by repository ID, recorded in a new lockfile revision 3. SLSA provenance checks now require the expected signer identity. This release also fixes regressions in `mise run --no-timings`, `cargo +nightly` and the `outdated`/`upgrade` version comparison, and speeds up shims and config loading.
  > 
  > ## Added
  > 
  > - **Per-tool `libc` for aqua tools.** On glibc Linux, mise prefers a …
- [v2026.9.15](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.15) 2026-09-27
  > `mise oci build` can now package tools installed by vfox plugins, and vfox plugins can repair an existing install when its tool options change. Shell prompts, `cd`, and settings loading are faster. Dotfiles `include`/`exclude` patterns now follow `.gitignore` rules for `*` and a leading `/`, which fixes a case where rollback could delete a live file.
  > 
  > ## Added
  > 
  > - **vfox tools in OCI images (experimental).** `mise oci build` used to reject every tool installed by a vfox plugin. It now builds those tools into the image, with one layer per tool plus one layer per plugin at `/mise/plugins/<name>/` …
- [v2026.9.14](https://github.com/jdx/mise/releases/tag/v2026.9.14) 2026-09-25
  > A folder inside any `conf.d` directory now loads as its own config fragment and serves as the config root for the files in it, which gives `[bootstrap].config_roots` users a direct migration path. `[dotfiles]` gains two GNU Stow-style options: relative symlinks and `dot-<name>` sources. Release metadata for any public github.com repo now comes from mise-versions, and the registry can require GitHub attestations for specific tools.
  > 
  > ## Added
  > 
  > - **conf.d folder fragments.** A folder in a global, system, or project `conf.d` directory now loads as a fragment. Relative paths, `{{ config_root }}`, a …

### Trivy Server — 🔴 update recommended

Template repo [RockinPaul/trivy_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/trivy_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/trivy-server)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image, used unmodified. Adds Railway environment plus a guard that refuses to start without TRIVY_TOKEN, because an empty token disables authentication entirely and upstream's own guidance for an exposed server is to configure one. No browser surface; the listing leads with the client command. Image and git tags are both plain semver with a v prefix on the git side. Apache-2.0. See guide \u00a75.30.

### QwenPaw — 🟠 review

Template repo [RockinPaul/qwenpaw_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/qwenpaw_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/qwenpaw-1)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image, used unmodified - only env vars and an entrypoint. Requires a login upstream leaves optional, and deliberately runs NO relay, because QwenPaw skips auth for 127.0.0.1/::1 and an in-container proxy would silently disable the login (measured 200 via relay vs 401 direct). Rebinding to :: was tried and reverted, since Python sets IPV6_V6ONLY and Railway's public proxy is IPv4. Auto-registration variables are write-once, and so is the agent language: upstream defaults to zh with no env var and no init flag, and its non-interactive init copies persona files with skip_existing=True, so the wrapper seeds config.json and runs init itself before handing off (QWENPAW_AGENT_LANGUAGE, default en). Apache-2.0. See guide §5.31.

### ReMe — 🟠 review

Template repo [RockinPaul/reme_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/reme_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-29) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/reme)

> First template with NO upstream image (ReMe ships only a pip package) and NO upstream authentication - upstream states the service layer has none, CORS allows any origin, jobs may write/move/delete files, and instructs users to place it behind an authenticated reverse proxy rather than exposing it directly. So Caddy holds a generated bcrypt credential as the only public listener while ReMe keeps its default 127.0.0.1 bind and service.host=0.0.0.0 is never set. All 149 resolved packages are hash-pinned in requirements.lock because without an upstream image a rebuild is a fresh resolution; regenerate on linux/amd64. reme-ai declares reme_studio with no version constraint, so the lock is what stops the UI drifting. Apache-2.0. See guide §5.32.

### Marmot — 🟡 minor drift

Template repo [RockinPaul/marmot_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/marmot_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-28) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/marmot)

> Thin wrapper over upstream's own multi-arch image plus su-exec. The entrypoint replaces Marmot's built-in admin:admin against a loopback-only listener before the public port opens, because upstream ships that credential and only tells you to change it after first login — on a public domain that would leave the catalog, the REST API and the MCP endpoint open to whoever tries the obvious pair. Image tags are semver WITHOUT a v prefix; the git tag has one, and upstream also publishes -previewN tags that the tag_pattern deliberately excludes. MARMOT_SERVER_ENCRYPTION_KEY must base64-decode to exactly 32 bytes, which ${{secret(N)}} cannot produce, so the key is derived as base64(sha256(MARMOT_ENCRYPTION_SEED)). Plugins (71 of them in 0.11.0, ~1.5 GB, no way to narrow the list) install from ghcr.io at every startup, so MARMOT_PLUGINS_DIR points at the 5120 MB volume to pay for it once; a bump can outgrow the volume while /readyz stays green, and the entrypoint clears the cache when the marmot version changes. MIT. See guide §5.34.

### PaddleOCR — 🟡 minor drift

Template repo [RockinPaul/paddleocr_railway_template](https://github.com/RockinPaul/paddleocr_railway_template) (last commit 2026-09-16) · [Railway listing](https://railway.com/deploy/paddleocr)

> Second template with no upstream image (PaddleOCR ships pip packages only) and no upstream authentication, so Caddy holds a generated credential and paddlex --serve is pinned to 127.0.0.1 rather than its 0.0.0.0 default. FIRST template with NO VOLUME: stateless, and the pipeline's 5 models (177 MB) are baked into the image because PaddleX downloads them when the SERVER starts, not lazily. The first inference then costs 98s versus ~4s warm, so the entrypoint runs a throwaway inference before the proxy opens. PADDLE_PDX_ENABLE_MKLDNN_BYDEFAULT=False is REQUIRED: oneDNN breaks every request on real x86 with PaddlePaddle 3.3.1, and a local emulated rehearsal cannot reproduce it. Apache-2.0. See guide §5.33.
